# API compatibility

Compatibility is tracked per command in an explicit, baseline-bound audited ledger. Direct registry tests establish API presence only. Verified requires an audit of the command's complete observable semantic contract on the supported Erlang term model, actual original OTP comparisons, and no known or unresolved semantic gaps. Selected-case or subcontract-only evidence remains Needs verification. Verification does not claim identical internal VM layout, timings or performance; APIs exposing resource values or options still require their complete observable contracts.

Pinned baseline: OTP-29.1.1 (`ad05823719d77c8faee87348ea39513d4e2f99c5`).

## 100% compatible

## Needs verification for 100% compatibility

### erlang

| Command | Compatibility | Audited scope | Evidence | Unresolved limits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `erlang:make_ref/0` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:register/2` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:whereis/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:unregister/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:link/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending Already-connected guest remote PID LINK output is accepted into finite connection-owned staging before the normal caller waits. Captured-generation LINK, local relation and local receiver priority commit together after actual encoding/capacity preflight. An accepted worker retains its ticket and entry lease across producer cancellation or kill; retained terminal EXIT follows LINK in order. Normal callers cooperatively admit existing owner signals/system queries while awaiting turn or capacity. With async_dist true the caller returns after bounded acceptance without that output wait. Duplicate LINK remains idempotent, link/2 options validate before mutation, and hard staging-cap refusal raises system_limit before relation installation. SDK staging and transport have separately finite accounted-byte/packet budgets; these are SDK resource bounds, not physical native buffer sizes. Accepted UNLINK remains connection-owned and only its guest wait admits caller signals. Pending/autoconnect, remote names/aliases, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK network peers, native scheduler/queue-phase identity and general native wire equality remain incomplete. Five actual generated-guest HOST controls cover normal/async turn/capacity ownership and hard-cap refusal. The separate pinned original325 collector remains unexecuted by this pipeline. |
| `erlang:unlink/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending Already-connected guest remote PID unlink suppresses the local relation before a captured-generation UNLINK_ID is accepted by the connection-owned worker. Accepted unlink output survives producer death and joins global output drain; ACK and stale EXIT identities preserve relink state. Duplicate unlink is idempotent; pending/autoconnect, a live SDK network peer and native wire equality remain incomplete. Caller-owned already-connected output waits now serially admit existing owner control signals and system queries while waiting for ticket turn or transport capacity, preserving ordinary message receive order and existing nested-wait guards. Cancellation or inbound kill settles producer reservations before completion. Accepted UNLINK workers remain connection-owned; only the guest wait admits its caller signals, without canceling retained output. Immediate nosuspend and accepted async staging do not gain capacity waits. Pending/autoconnect, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK peers and native scheduler/wire equality remain incomplete. |
| `erlang:monitor/2` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:demonitor/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:process_flag/2` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending async_dist accepts only true/false, returns the previous per-process boolean and defaults false; a spawned child does not inherit it. Connected PID output ownership follows the captured flag. Pending/autoconnect and async_dist spawn options remain incomplete; process_flag/3 is unchanged. Per-process error_handler accepts an atom and returns the previous handler; children default to error_handler. Missing supported registered/source MFA calls can invoke the selected undefined_function/3 in the original process with original arguments, normal source ownership and bounded forwarded-error frames. Missing handler retains original undef; valid callees raising undef and invalid registered-name sends are not intercepted. Kernel autoload/code-server behavior, breakpoints, arbitrary raw BEAM imports and whole erlang compatibility remain incomplete. Original327 ordinary comparison is a separate pending gate. Restored local source fun invocation can select the executing process error_handler undefined_lambda/3 with the original wrapper and arguments after source retirement and no CURRENT code; active old bodies, pending purge control pumping, source callback leases and relevant factory publication validation are within the reviewed supported slice. Compatible source relink requires a pre-registered factory. Fresh CURRENT without a factory resolver, direct foreign captured-image transfer, default handler autoload, arbitrary raw BEAM and whole erlang parity remain partial. Current328 original comparison is a separate gate. |
| `erlang:exit/1` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending |
| `erlang:exit/2` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending Already-connected guest remote PID explicit exits publish captured-generation EXIT2 (normal opcode 26 or legacy opcode 8) or ALTACT (opcode 37, EXIT flag16 plus priority flag1). Explicit exits do not advance sequence tracing, attach tokens, or perform pseudo-send tracing; exit/3 and exit_signal/3 validate the complete options list and preserve duplicate priority semantics before remote mutation. Accepted async_dist output has finite staged and transport budgets; normal producer-owned output retains cancellation and generation checks. Local and remote exit aliases preserve their existing public contracts. Pending/autoconnect, remote names/aliases, a live SDK network peer and general native wire equality remain incomplete. Caller-owned already-connected output waits now serially admit existing owner control signals and system queries while waiting for ticket turn or transport capacity, preserving ordinary message receive order and existing nested-wait guards. Cancellation or inbound kill settles producer reservations before completion. Accepted UNLINK workers remain connection-owned; only the guest wait admits its caller signals, without canceling retained output. Immediate nosuspend and accepted async staging do not gain capacity waits. Pending/autoconnect, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK peers and native scheduler/wire equality remain incomplete. |

| `erlang:send/3` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Registered guest already-connected remote PID sends use owned ordered publication, producer cancellation and generation isolation. Full option validation precedes state checks; connected noconnect may send. Immediate nosuspend conservatively returns WouldSuspend when capacity or captured sequential-trace state prevents admission, without advancing the token. Negotiated sender, trace and priority controls are bounded SDK adaptations; native scheduler phase parity is not claimed. Disconnected/pending autoconnect, remote named destinations/aliases and synchronous embedding helpers remain incomplete. SDK HOST/controller and original-only observations are separate; no live SDK-peer or native wire equality is claimed. With process_flag(async_dist,true), already-connected PID sends use bounded entry-owned accepted packet staging: accepted output survives sender death, retains original order and contributes staged plus transport AccountedBytes to dist_get_stat SendPend. False retains producer-owned319 behavior. A full independent staging budget raises system_limit before acceptance, including send_nosuspend; it is an SDK cap, not a native busy threshold or physical-memory measurement. Original new flag cases and live SDK-peer comparison remain separate gates. Caller-owned already-connected output waits now serially admit existing owner control signals and system queries while waiting for ticket turn or transport capacity, preserving ordinary message receive order and existing nested-wait guards. Cancellation or inbound kill settles producer reservations before completion. Accepted UNLINK workers remain connection-owned; only the guest wait admits its caller signals, without canceling retained output. Immediate nosuspend and accepted async staging do not gain capacity waits. Pending/autoconnect, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK peers and native scheduler/wire equality remain incomplete. |

| `erlang:link/2` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending Already-connected guest remote PID LINK output is accepted into finite connection-owned staging before the normal caller waits. Captured-generation LINK, local relation and local receiver priority commit together after actual encoding/capacity preflight. An accepted worker retains its ticket and entry lease across producer cancellation or kill; retained terminal EXIT follows LINK in order. Normal callers cooperatively admit existing owner signals/system queries while awaiting turn or capacity. With async_dist true the caller returns after bounded acceptance without that output wait. Duplicate LINK remains idempotent, link/2 options validate before mutation, and hard staging-cap refusal raises system_limit before relation installation. SDK staging and transport have separately finite accounted-byte/packet budgets; these are SDK resource bounds, not physical native buffer sizes. Accepted UNLINK remains connection-owned and only its guest wait admits caller signals. Pending/autoconnect, remote names/aliases, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK network peers, native scheduler/queue-phase identity and general native wire equality remain incomplete. Five actual generated-guest HOST controls cover normal/async turn/capacity ownership and hard-cap refusal. The separate pinned original325 collector remains unexecuted by this pipeline. |
| `erlang:exit/3` | Needs verification for 100% compatibility | Complete command contract against the pinned OTP baseline has not yet been independently audited. | Pending audit | Single direct contract case plus feature regressions; complete error/options and OTP differential verification pending Already-connected guest remote PID explicit exits publish captured-generation EXIT2 (normal opcode 26 or legacy opcode 8) or ALTACT (opcode 37, EXIT flag16 plus priority flag1). Explicit exits do not advance sequence tracing, attach tokens, or perform pseudo-send tracing; exit/3 and exit_signal/3 validate the complete options list and preserve duplicate priority semantics before remote mutation. Accepted async_dist output has finite staged and transport budgets; normal producer-owned output retains cancellation and generation checks. Local and remote exit aliases preserve their existing public contracts. Pending/autoconnect, remote names/aliases, a live SDK network peer and general native wire equality remain incomplete. Caller-owned already-connected output waits now serially admit existing owner control signals and system queries while waiting for ticket turn or transport capacity, preserving ordinary message receive order and existing nested-wait guards. Cancellation or inbound kill settles producer reservations before completion. Accepted UNLINK workers remain connection-owned; only the guest wait admits its caller signals, without canceling retained output. Immediate nosuspend and accepted async staging do not gain capacity waits. Explicit exits do not advance sequence tracing, attach tokens, or perform pseudo-send tracing; exit/3 and exit_signal/3 validate the complete options list and preserve duplicate priority semantics before remote mutation. Accepted async_dist output has finite staged and transport budgets; normal producer-owned output retains cancellation and generation checks. Local and remote exit aliases preserve their existing public contracts. Pending/autoconnect, remote names/aliases, a live SDK network peer and general native wire equality remain incomplete. Caller-owned already-connected output waits now serially admit existing owner control signals and system queries while waiting for ticket turn or transport capacity, preserving ordinary message receive order and existing nested-wait guards. Cancellation or inbound kill settles producer reservations before completion. Accepted UNLINK workers remain connection-owned; only the guest wait admits its caller signals, without canceling retained output. Immediate nosuspend and accepted async staging do not gain capacity waits. Pending/autoconnect, synchronous embedding setters, complete inbound priority, live SDK peers and native scheduler/wire equality remain incomplete. |
